# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change вЂ” all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** вЂ” check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer the visual companion just-in-time** вЂ” NOT upfront. The first time a question would genuinely be clearer shown than described, offer it then (its own message); on approval its browser tab opens for you. If no visual question ever arises, never offer it. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** вЂ” one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** вЂ” with trade-offs and your recommendation

Blackwell Compatibility Guide

Blackwell Compatibility Guide for CUDA Applications

The guide to building CUDA applications for Blackwell GPUs

# 1. Blackwell Architecture Compatibility

## 1.1. About this Document

This application note, Blackwell Architecture Compatibility Guide for CUDA Applications, is intended to help developers ensure that their NVIDIA^{®} CUDA^{®} applications will run on the NVIDIA^{®} Blackwell architecture based GPUs. This document provides guidance to developers who are familiar with programming in CUDA C++ and want to make sure that their software applications are compatible with Blackwell architecture.

## 1.2. Application Compatibility on Blackwell Architecture

A CUDA application binary (with one or more GPU kernels) can contain the compiled GPU code in two forms, binary cubin objects and forward-compatible PTX assembly for each kernel. Both cubin and PTX are generated for a certain target compute capability. A cubin generated for a certain compute capability is supported to run on any GPU with the same major revision and same or higher minor revision <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="4941" />---------------------------
Release Notes (https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.0/pdf/CUDA_Toolkit_Release_Notes.pdf)

[19] assistant: Целевая сборка будет такой:

- A100 40 GB — native `sm_80`
- H100 — native `sm_90`
- L4 — native `sm_89`
- RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB (полагаю, это ваша «9000 RTX») — native `sm_120`
- плюс PTX fallback для прочих новых карт

Для Blackwell нужен отдельный архив, собранный новым CUDA Toolkit: NVIDIA указывает для RTX Blackwell compute capability 12.0, а fat binary позволяет рантайму автоматически выбрать подходящий cubin. [Таблица GPU NVIDIA](https://developer.nvidia.com/cuda/gpus), [руководство совместимости Blackwell](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.0/blackwell-compatibility-guide/index.html).

# Public CayleyPy 2xT4 Solver and Results Ingest Design

Date: 2026-07-28
Status: approved design, pending written-spec review

## 1. Objective

Deliver a public, copy-and-run Kaggle notebook for standard CayleyPy permutation-puzzle competitions on exactly two NVIDIA T4 GPUs. A user attaches the standard `puzzle_info.json`, `test.csv`, and `sample_submission.csv`, supplies one supported MLP checkpoint, chooses a puzzle range and search modes, and runs the existing MultiGPUBeamSearch solver.

Validated results are also submitted best-effort to a public Cloudflare ingestion service. The service must safely accept at least 100 concurrent notebook publishers, preserve every accepted raw payload only until a confirmed GitHub commit, validate solutions, retain authorship and run provenance in GitHub, and publish append-only result records to `TryDotAtwo/cayleypy-beam-results` without exposing a GitHub write token.

The work is split into two independently testable deliverables:

1. Public Kaggle notebook plus repository helper CLI.
2. Cloudflare Results Ingest plus GitHub App publisher and repository CI validation.

[30] assistant: Worker найден. Он находится в ветке `codex/cayleypy-results-ingest`:

- endpoint: `POST /v1/results`
- staging: `https://cayleypy-results-ingest-staging.tupa-expert.workers.dev`
- после проверки Worker публикует результаты через GitHub App в `TryDotAtwo/cayleypy-beam-results`
- клиентская публикация уже проверена: тестовый запрос получил `202`
- API анонимный, но строго валидирует схему и независимо воспроизводит решение

Варианты поставки:

1. Рекомендую отдельный архив на каждую архитектуру: `sm75`, `sm80`, `sm86`, `sm89`, `sm90`, `sm120`. В каждом только соответствующий native cubin, launcher, NCCL/CUDA runtime-библиотеки, веса и Megaminx-данные. Архив меньше, а неподходящая карта отклоняется явно.
2. Один большой архив со всеми native cubin. Скачивать проще, но он заметно тяжелее, сложнее диагностировать зависимости и обновлять Blackwell отдельно.
3. JIT-сборка на кластере. Не требует матрицы релизов, но противоречит цели «сразу работает» и создаёт зависимость от toolkit/compiler, поэтому исключаю.

Предлагаемая архитектура первой части:

- отдельная ветка `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release`, созданная в чистом worktree от `origin/main`;
- GitHub Releases содержит шесть архивов для Ubuntu 22.04+ x86_64;
- `./run.sh` спрашивает только список GPU и beam width либо принимает:
  `./run.sh --gpus 0,1,2,3 --beam 1000000000`;
- launcher проверяет одинаковую поддерживаемую архитектуру всех выбранных карт, VRAM, драйвер, свободный диск и сеть;
- смешивать разные архитектуры в одном запуске нельзя;
- неизвестный SM, отсутствующая библиотека или несовместимый драйвер — немедленная понятная ошибка, никаких fallback/PTX/JIT;
- puzzle data, экспортированные веса и проверенные профили входят в release;
- локальный результат всегда сохраняется; затем существующий клиент отправляет его Worker, а Worker публикует в GitHub.

Такое разделение и поведение запуска подходят? После подтверждения уточню только политику выбора Megaminx-задач и представлю оставшуюся часть дизайна.

[33] assistant: Принял. `run.sh` будет именно SLURM-launcher:

```bash
./run.sh --gpus 0,1,2,3 --beam 1000000000
```

Он:

1. Проверяет выбранные GPU и соответствие архива архитектуре.
2. Формирует и отправляет `sbatch`.
3. В SLURM job запускает ровно один rank на GPU через `torchrun --nproc-per-node=4`.
4. Передаёт SLURM/NCCL rendezvous-параметры, сохраняет job ID и логи.
5. После решения валидирует результат на CPU и отправляет его существующему Cloudflare Worker.
6. Ошибка SLURM, NCCL, архитектуры или публикации показывается явно; вычисленный результат не теряется.

Параметры конкретного кластера (`partition`, `account`, `qos`, каталог scratch) вынесем в один небольшой `cluster.env`, который настраивается один раз. В обычном запуске пользователь указывает только GPU и beam.

Следующий выбор: какую Megaminx-задачу запускать без дополнительного аргумента — фиксированную текущую задачу `p900`, весь подготовленный список по очереди или получать следующую свободную задачу от отдельного API? Текущий Worker принимает результаты, но задания пока не распределяет.

[35] assistant: Понял: сохраняем существующую проверенную семантику «один SLURM job — один puzzle», включая опциональный reflected-проход; меняется только упаковка и UX. Сейчас сверяю точные параметры действующих cluster-скриптов, чтобы публичный launcher был тонкой оболочкой над тем же execution path, а не новым вариантом solver-а.

#!/bin/bash
#SBATCH --job-name=beam8a100-reflect
#SBATCH --nodes=1
#SBATCH --ntasks=1
#SBATCH --gres=gpu:8
#SBATCH --cpus-per-task=8
#SBATCH --time=24:00:00

set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="${SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR:-$(pwd)}"
BEAM_COMMON_SH="${BEAM_COMMON_SH:-${SCRIPT_DIR}/mephi_8xa100_common.sh}"
if [ ! -f "${BEAM_COMMON_SH}" ]; then
  echo "missing_common_script=${BEAM_COMMON_SH}"
  exit 2
fi
source "${BEAM_COMMON_SH}"

beam_setup_paths

After the Stream1 job finishes, launch the production pipeline sweep:

```bash
sbatch -p kaf12 tune_8xa100_pipeline.sh
```

After the pipeline sweep writes `logs/best_pipeline.env`, launch the selected
configuration:

```bash
sbatch -p kaf12 start_8xa100_best.sh
```

The sweep scripts write:

- `logs/best_stream1.env`: best isolated `BEAM_B_MICRO` and
  `BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY`.
- `logs/tuning_<job_id>/pipeline_sweep_<job_id>.tsv`: per-config pass/fail and
  average full-depth runtime through depth `12`.
- `logs/best_pipeline.env`: fastest passing production config found by the
  pipeline sweep.

Check the job:

```bash
scontrol show job <job_id>
cat /mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/slurm-<job_id>.out
tail -n 200 /mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/logs/production_runner_p992_d12_b260m_<job_id>.log
```

Run parameters:

- puzzle: `992`
- depth: `12`
- global beam: `260000000`
- GPUs/ranks: `8`
- CUDA architecture: `sm_80`
- solved-neighborhood radius: `5`
- history mode: `static_hybrid`
- Stream3 prediction stats: enabled at `predict_stats_p992_b260m_d12.jsonl`

Cleanup behavior:

- Always removes only `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/build-a100`.
- Removes only `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/history` after a successful
  run.
- Keeps `history` after a failed run for diagnostics.
- Never removes `repo`, `logs`, `slurm-*.out`, `predict_stats`, or
  `/mnt/pool/3/vokirova/cutlass` during normal job cleanup.

Network rule:

- `start.sh` does not run `git clone`, `git fetch`, or `git reset` inside the
  compute job. Prepare `repo/` and `/mnt/pool/3/vokirova/cutlass` from the
  visible `basis` shell before `sbatch`.



[38] assistant: Тогда итоговый UX повторяет текущий кластерный процесс, но убирает подготовку окружения и компиляцию.

### Запуск

```bash
./run.sh \
  --gpus 0,1,2,3 \
  --beam 1000000000 \
  --puzzle 900 \
  --reflect off
```

Режимы:

- `--reflect off` — только исходный puzzle;
- `--reflect after` — решить исходный, построить reflected-state, решить его и преобразовать результат обратно;
- `--reflect only --original-solution ...` — существующий reflected-only сценарий;
- если `--puzzle` пропущен, используется `DEFAULT_PUZZLE_ID` из `cluster.env`;
- `--reflect` по умолчанию `off`.

`run.sh` находится на login node, валидирует параметры и вызывает `sbatch`. Внутри job запускается текущий production path через `torchrun`, один rank на каждый GPU из `--gpus`. Никакой компиляции, загрузки CUTLASS или изменения репозитория на compute node.

### Содержимое архива

Каждый архив содержит только:

```text
megaminx-sm80/
  run.sh
  cluster.env.example
  bin/production_runner
  lib/                    # требуемые runtime libraries
  data/                   # Megaminx puzzle data
  weights/
  profiles/
  scripts/job.sh
  scripts/validate_and_publish.py
  MANIFEST.json
  SHA256SUMS
  README.md
```

Отдельные релизы: `sm75`, `sm80`, `sm86`, `sm89`, `sm90`, `sm120`. У каждого бинарника ровно одна native-архитектура, без PTX. Проверка через `cuobjdump` входит в release gate.

### Поведение job

1. SLURM выделяет количество GPU, равное длине `--gpus`.
2. Проверяется, что все карты имеют SM архива и достаточно VRAM.
3. Beam выравнивается тем же способом, что сейчас; requested и effective значения записываются отдельно.
4. Запускается один puzzle.
5. При `after` запускается существующая последовательность original → reflected → обратная проверка.
6. Все найденные пути независимо replay-валидируются на CPU.
7. Артефакты остаются локально даже при сетевой ошибке.
8. Валидный результат отправляется в найденный `POST /v1/results`; Worker самостоятельно публикует его в GitHub.
9. Неуспешную публикацию можно повторить командой `./run.sh --publish-only <run-dir>` без повторного solve.

### Ветка и релизы

Работа пойдёт в чистом worktree и отдельной ветке `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release`. В неё попадут только portable launcher, упаковка, release workflow, тесты и документация — без текущих локальных экспериментов, build-каталогов и приватных cluster paths. Precompiled-архивы будут прикрепляться к GitHub Release, а не храниться бинарниками в Git.

Проверки перед публикацией:

- shell/CLI-тесты и mock `sbatch`;
- проверка выбора GPU/rank и запрета mixed-SM;
- `cuobjdump` подтверждает только требуемый native cubin и отсутствие PTX;
- smoke каждого архива на соответствующей карте;
- original/reflected replay;
- тест отправки в staging Worker и появления записи в GitHub;
- скан архива на токены, приватные пути, логи и лишние файлы.

Если этот дизайн утверждаете, я запишу и закоммичу спецификацию в отдельной ветке; после вашего просмотра перейду к подробному implementation plan и реализации.

name: using-git-worktrees
description: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback

# Using Git Worktrees

## Overview

Ensure work happens in an isolated workspace. Prefer your platform's native worktree tools. Fall back to manual git worktrees only when no native tool is available.

**Core principle:** Detect existing isolation first. Then use native tools. Then fall back to git. Never fight the harness.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the using-git-worktrees skill to set up an isolated workspace."

## Step 0: Detect Existing Isolation

**Before creating anything, check if you are already in an isolated workspace.**

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

**Submodule guard:** `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` is also true inside git submodules. Before concluding "already in a worktree," verify you are not in a submodule:

```bash
# If this returns a path, you're in a submodule, not a worktree вЂ” treat as normal repo
git rev-parse --show-superproject-working-tree 2>/dev/null
```

**If `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` (and not a submodule):** You are already in a linked worktree. Skip to Step 2 (Project Setup). Do NOT create another worktree.

Report with branch state:
- On a branch: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` on branch `<name>`."
- Detached HEAD: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` (detached HEAD, externally managed). Branch creation needed at finish time."

**If `GIT_DIR == GIT_COMMON` (or in a submodule):** You are in a normal repo checkout.

Has the user already indicated their worktree preference in your instructions? If not, ask for<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1670" />ification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence в‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter в‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion в‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
вњ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
вќЊ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
вњ… Write в†’ Run (pass) в†’ Revert fix в†’ Run (MUST FAIL) в†’ Restore в†’ Run (pass)
вќЊ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
вњ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
вќЊ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
вњ… Re-read plan в†’ Create checklist в†’ Verify each в†’ Report gaps or completion
вќЊ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
вњ… Agent reports success в†’ Check VCS diff в†’ Verify changes в†’ Report actual state
вќЊ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change вЂ” all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** вЂ” check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer the visual companion just-in-time** вЂ” NOT upfront. The first time a question would genuinely be clearer shown than described, offer it then (its own message); on approval its browser tab opens for you. If no visual question ever arises, never offer it. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** вЂ” one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** вЂ” with trade-offs and your recommendation

Blackwell Compatibility Guide

Blackwell Compatibility Guide for CUDA Applications

The guide to building CUDA applications for Blackwell GPUs

# 1. Blackwell Architecture Compatibility

## 1.1. About this Document

This application note, Blackwell Architecture Compatibility Guide for CUDA Applications, is intended to help developers ensure that their NVIDIA^{®} CUDA^{®} applications will run on the NVIDIA^{®} Blackwell architecture based GPUs. This document provides guidance to developers who are familiar with programming in CUDA C++ and want to make sure that their software applications are compatible with Blackwell architecture.

## 1.2. Application Compatibility on Blackwell Architecture

A CUDA application binary (with one or more GPU kernels) can contain the compiled GPU code in two forms, binary cubin objects and forward-compatible PTX assembly for each kernel. Both cubin and PTX are generated for a certain target compute capability. A cubin generated for a certain compute capability is supported to run on any GPU with the same major revision and same or higher minor revision <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="4941" />---------------------------
Release Notes (https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.0/pdf/CUDA_Toolkit_Release_Notes.pdf)

[19] assistant: Целевая сборка будет такой:

- A100 40 GB — native `sm_80`
- H100 — native `sm_90`
- L4 — native `sm_89`
- RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB (полагаю, это ваша «9000 RTX») — native `sm_120`
- плюс PTX fallback для прочих новых карт

Для Blackwell нужен отдельный архив, собранный новым CUDA Toolkit: NVIDIA указывает для RTX Blackwell compute capability 12.0, а fat binary позволяет рантайму автоматически выбрать подходящий cubin. [Таблица GPU NVIDIA](https://developer.nvidia.com/cuda/gpus), [руководство совместимости Blackwell](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.0/blackwell-compatibility-guide/index.html).

# Public CayleyPy 2xT4 Solver and Results Ingest Design

Date: 2026-07-28
Status: approved design, pending written-spec review

## 1. Objective

Deliver a public, copy-and-run Kaggle notebook for standard CayleyPy permutation-puzzle competitions on exactly two NVIDIA T4 GPUs. A user attaches the standard `puzzle_info.json`, `test.csv`, and `sample_submission.csv`, supplies one supported MLP checkpoint, chooses a puzzle range and search modes, and runs the existing MultiGPUBeamSearch solver.

Validated results are also submitted best-effort to a public Cloudflare ingestion service. The service must safely accept at least 100 concurrent notebook publishers, preserve every accepted raw payload only until a confirmed GitHub commit, validate solutions, retain authorship and run provenance in GitHub, and publish append-only result records to `TryDotAtwo/cayleypy-beam-results` without exposing a GitHub write token.

The work is split into two independently testable deliverables:

1. Public Kaggle notebook plus repository helper CLI.
2. Cloudflare Results Ingest plus GitHub App publisher and repository CI validation.

[30] assistant: Worker найден. Он находится в ветке `codex/cayleypy-results-ingest`:

- endpoint: `POST /v1/results`
- staging: `https://cayleypy-results-ingest-staging.tupa-expert.workers.dev`
- после проверки Worker публикует результаты через GitHub App в `TryDotAtwo/cayleypy-beam-results`
- клиентская публикация уже проверена: тестовый запрос получил `202`
- API анонимный, но строго валидирует схему и независимо воспроизводит решение

Варианты поставки:

1. Рекомендую отдельный архив на каждую архитектуру: `sm75`, `sm80`, `sm86`, `sm89`, `sm90`, `sm120`. В каждом только соответствующий native cubin, launcher, NCCL/CUDA runtime-библиотеки, веса и Megaminx-данные. Архив меньше, а неподходящая карта отклоняется явно.
2. Один большой архив со всеми native cubin. Скачивать проще, но он заметно тяжелее, сложнее диагностировать зависимости и обновлять Blackwell отдельно.
3. JIT-сборка на кластере. Не требует матрицы релизов, но противоречит цели «сразу работает» и создаёт зависимость от toolkit/compiler, поэтому исключаю.

Предлагаемая архитектура первой части:

- отдельная ветка `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release`, созданная в чистом worktree от `origin/main`;
- GitHub Releases содержит шесть архивов для Ubuntu 22.04+ x86_64;
- `./run.sh` спрашивает только список GPU и beam width либо принимает:
  `./run.sh --gpus 0,1,2,3 --beam 1000000000`;
- launcher проверяет одинаковую поддерживаемую архитектуру всех выбранных карт, VRAM, драйвер, свободный диск и сеть;
- смешивать разные архитектуры в одном запуске нельзя;
- неизвестный SM, отсутствующая библиотека или несовместимый драйвер — немедленная понятная ошибка, никаких fallback/PTX/JIT;
- puzzle data, экспортированные веса и проверенные профили входят в release;
- локальный результат всегда сохраняется; затем существующий клиент отправляет его Worker, а Worker публикует в GitHub.

Такое разделение и поведение запуска подходят? После подтверждения уточню только политику выбора Megaminx-задач и представлю оставшуюся часть дизайна.

[33] assistant: Принял. `run.sh` будет именно SLURM-launcher:

```bash
./run.sh --gpus 0,1,2,3 --beam 1000000000
```

Он:

1. Проверяет выбранные GPU и соответствие архива архитектуре.
2. Формирует и отправляет `sbatch`.
3. В SLURM job запускает ровно один rank на GPU через `torchrun --nproc-per-node=4`.
4. Передаёт SLURM/NCCL rendezvous-параметры, сохраняет job ID и логи.
5. После решения валидирует результат на CPU и отправляет его существующему Cloudflare Worker.
6. Ошибка SLURM, NCCL, архитектуры или публикации показывается явно; вычисленный результат не теряется.

Параметры конкретного кластера (`partition`, `account`, `qos`, каталог scratch) вынесем в один небольшой `cluster.env`, который настраивается один раз. В обычном запуске пользователь указывает только GPU и beam.

Следующий выбор: какую Megaminx-задачу запускать без дополнительного аргумента — фиксированную текущую задачу `p900`, весь подготовленный список по очереди или получать следующую свободную задачу от отдельного API? Текущий Worker принимает результаты, но задания пока не распределяет.

if [ ! -f "${BEAM_COMMON_SH}" ]; then

[38] assistant: Тогда итоговый UX повторяет текущий кластерный процесс, но убирает подготовку окружения и компиляцию.

### Запуск

```bash
./run.sh \
  --gpus 0,1,2,3 \
  --beam 1000000000 \
  --puzzle 900 \
  --reflect off
```

Режимы:

- `--reflect off` — только исходный puzzle;
- `--reflect after` — решить исходный, построить reflected-state, решить его и преобразовать результат обратно;
- `--reflect only --original-solution ...` — существующий reflected-only сценарий;
- если `--puzzle` пропущен, используется `DEFAULT_PUZZLE_ID` из `cluster.env`;
- `--reflect` по умолчанию `off`.

`run.sh` находится на login node, валидирует параметры и вызывает `sbatch`. Внутри job запускается текущий production path через `torchrun`, один rank на каждый GPU из `--gpus`. Никакой компиляции, загрузки CUTLASS или изменения репозитория на compute node.

### Содержимое архива

Каждый архив содержит только:

```text
megaminx-sm80/
  run.sh
  cluster.env.example
  bin/production_runner
  lib/                    # требуемые runtime libraries
  data/                   # Megaminx puzzle data
  weights/
  profiles/
  scripts/job.sh
  scripts/validate_and_publish.py
  MANIFEST.json
  SHA256SUMS
  README.md
```

Отдельные релизы: `sm75`, `sm80`, `sm86`, `sm89`, `sm90`, `sm120`. У каждого бинарника ровно одна native-архитектура, без PTX. Проверка через `cuobjdump` входит в release gate.

### Поведение job

1. SLURM выделяет количество GPU, равное длине `--gpus`.
2. Проверяется, что все карты имеют SM архива и достаточно VRAM.
3. Beam выравнивается тем же способом, что сейчас; requested и effective значения записываются отдельно.
4. Запускается один puzzle.
5. При `after` запускается существующая последовательность original → reflected → обратная проверка.
6. Все найденные пути независимо replay-валидируются на CPU.
7. Артефакты остаются локально даже при сетевой ошибке.
8. Валидный результат отправляется в найденный `POST /v1/results`; Worker самостоятельно публикует его в GitHub.
9. Неуспешную публикацию можно повторить командой `./run.sh --publish-only <run-dir>` без повторного solve.

### Ветка и релизы

Работа пойдёт в чистом worktree и отдельной ветке `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release`. В неё попадут только portable launcher, упаковка, release workflow, тесты и документация — без текущих локальных экспериментов, build-каталогов и приватных cluster paths. Precompiled-архивы будут прикрепляться к GitHub Release, а не храниться бинарниками в Git.

Проверки перед публикацией:

- shell/CLI-тесты и mock `sbatch`;
- проверка выбора GPU/rank и запрета mixed-SM;
- `cuobjdump` подтверждает только требуемый native cubin и отсутствие PTX;
- smoke каждого архива на соответствующей карте;
- original/reflected replay;
- тест отправки в staging Worker и появления записи в GitHub;
- скан архива на токены, приватные пути, логи и лишние файлы.

Если этот дизайн утверждаете, я запишу и закоммичу спецификацию в отдельной ветке; после вашего просмотра перейду к подробному implementation plan и реализации.

---
name: using-git-worktrees
description: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback
---

# Using Git Worktrees

## Overview

Ensure work happens in an isolated workspace. Prefer your platform's native worktree tools. Fall back to manual git worktrees only when no native tool is available.

**Core principle:** Detect existing isolation first. Then use native tools. Then fall back to git. Never fight the harness.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the using-git-worktrees skill to set up an isolated workspace."

## Step 0: Detect Existing Isolation

**Before creating anything, check if you are already in an isolated workspace.**

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

**Submodule guard:** `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` is also true inside git submodules. Before concluding "already in a worktree," verify you are not in a submodule:

```bash
# If this returns a path, you're in a submodule, not a worktree вЂ” treat as normal repo
git rev-parse --show-superproject-working-tree 2>/dev/null
```

**If `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` (and not a submodule):** You are already in a linked worktree. Skip to Step 2 (Project Setup). Do NOT create another worktree.

Report with branch state:
- On a branch: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` on branch `<name>`."
- Detached HEAD: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` (detached HEAD, externally managed). Branch creation needed at finish time."

**If `GIT_DIR == GIT_COMMON` (or in a submodule):** You are in a normal repo checkout.

Has the user already indicated their worktree preference in your instructions? If not, ask for<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1670" />ification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence в‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter в‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion в‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
вњ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
вќЊ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
вњ… Write в†’ Run (pass) в†’ Revert fix в†’ Run (MUST FAIL) в†’ Restore в†’ Run (pass)
вќЊ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
вњ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
вќЊ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
вњ… Re-read plan в†’ Create checklist в†’ Verify each в†’ Report gaps or completion
вќЊ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
вњ… Agent reports success в†’ Check VCS diff в†’ Verify changes в†’ Report actual state
вќЊ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

# Changelog

## 2026-06-20
- Added `BEAM_SOLVE_BUCKET_MODE` for ordinary solve-to-center runs that keep
  searching after the first hit. Stream2 can now record solved candidates
  without setting `stop_flag`; the runner completes the hit depth, completes
  `BEAM_SOLVE_BUCKET_EXTRA_DEPTHS` more depths, gathers solved buckets from all
  ranks, reconstructs every stored solution, and writes per-length summaries.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_bucket.sh`, a 8-GPU IHES launcher that
  selects known length-23 puzzles from `solv_uniq.csv`, runs original and
  reflected solve-bucket passes, and writes a compact TSV with the best found
  original-facing solution for each variant.
- Changed resident solution-repair task ordering to process higher puzzle ids
  first. Parsed solution rows are now stably sorted by descending `puzzle_id`,
  so `1000, 999, ...` are prioritized while preserving per-puzzle solution
  order.
- Added an explicit `BEAM_STREAM1_MODE=uniform` runtime mode for repair/debug
  runs. In this mode Stream1 model inference is not launched; the captured
  ring-slot graph fills the score ring with zero scores so selection behaves as
  an unscored BFS frontier subject only to the configured beam width and
  dedup/final materialization.
- Fixed distributed repair result reconstruction so rank 0 is always the
  reconstruction controller after a global solved stop. Previously rank 0 could
  write `segment_solved=1` with `segment_len=-1` when another rank owned the
  solved candidate, which made resident repair TSV aggregation report false
# Prompt History

- User clarified that the solve-bucket launcher must run both original and

## 2026-06-13

Supported Native Artifacts\n+\n+Release automation produces one Linux x86_64 archive for each native target:\n+\n+| Archive target | Intended GPUs |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `sm75` | T4 |\n+| `sm80` | A100, including 40 GB |\n+| `sm86` | RTX 30-series |\n+| `sm89` | RTX 40-series and L4 |\n+| `sm90` | H100 |\n+| `sm120` | RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB class |\n+\n+Each production runner contains cubin for exactly its named SM. It contains no\n+PTX. A release gate inspects the executable with `cuobjdump` and rejects an\n+archive that contains PTX, lacks the requested cubin, or contains another SM.\n+An unknown or mismatched device fails before `sbatch` when visible on the login\n+node and again inside the allocation before torchrun.\n+\n+The baseline platform is a current x86_64 Linux distribution compatible with\n+Ubuntu 22.04 or newer. The archive includes the solver's redistributable runtime\n+libraries. It does not bundle an NVIDIA driver. The manifest declares the\n+minimum driver, glibc, archive SM, build commit, dependency versions, and hashes.\n+\n+## 4. Archive Contract\n+\n+Each archive contains only the portable runtime payload:\n+\n+```text\n+megaminx-smXX/\n+  run.sh\n+  cluster.env.example\n+  bin/production_runner\n+  lib/\n+  data/\n+  weights/\n+  profiles/\n+  scripts/job.sh\n+  scripts/preflight.sh\n+  scripts/validate_and_publish.py\n+  MANIFEST.json\n+  SHA256SUMS\n+  README.md\n+```\n+\n+Source trees, compilers, CUTLASS sources, build directories, caches, benchmark\n+scratch, private paths, credentials, and historical logs are forbidden. Model\n+weights and puzzle data are versioned by SHA-256 in `MANIFEST.json`.\n+\n+## 5. User Interface\n+\n+Required options:\n+\n+- `--gpus <comma-separated ids>`: one or more local GPU ids.\n+- `--beam <positive integer>`: requested global beam width.\n+- `--puzzle <nonnegative integer>`: exactly one puzzle id.\n+\n+Omitting `--puzzle` is always an error. The launcher prints\n+`missing required --puzzle; specify one Megaminx puzzle id` and performs no\n+`sbatch` call. There is no default puzzle id.\n+\n+Optional public options:\n+\n+- `--reflect off|after|only`, default `off`.\n+- `--original-solution <path>` required only for `--reflect only`.\n+- `--depth <positive integer>` when overriding the release profile default.\n+- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving again.\n+- `--dry-run` to print the validated `sbatch` request without submitting it.\n+\n+Unknown options and invalid enum values fail closed. Secrets are neither CLI\n+arguments nor environment dumps. Cluster-specific SLURM settings live in a\n+small `cluster.env` configured once: partition, account, QoS, wall time, CPU,\n+RAM, and scratch root. Runtime solver tuning remains in signed/versioned\n+per-architecture profiles rather than normal user input.\n+\n+## 6. SLURM and torchrun Flow\n+\n+`run.sh` is a login-node submitter, not the compute payload. It validates the\n+archive hashes, CLI, requested GPU count, cluster configuration, writable\n+output path, and available commands, then submits `scripts/job.sh` with\n+`sbatch`. It prints the parsed SLURM job id and exact run directory.\n+\n+The job requests a single node and a GPU count equal to the number of ids. It\n+maps the selected allocation to `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, sets one rank per GPU,\n+and invokes `python -m torch.distributed.run --nproc-per-node=<gpu-count>\n+--no-python` around the prebuilt production runner. Rendezvous and NCCL ids are\n+unique to the SLURM job. Rank 0 streams to the main log; every other rank has a\n+separate durable log.\n+\n+Inside the allocation, preflight verifies:\n+\n+1. the exact number of visible devices;\n+2. identical compute capability equal to the archive SM;\n+3. driver/runtime compatibility and required shared libraries;\n+4. per-device VRAM and derived capacity safety for the requested beam;\n+5. local data, weight, profile, and executable hashes;\n+6. scratch and history capacity before launching ranks.\n+\n+There is no alternative execution path after a failed check.\n+\n+## 7. One-puzzle and Reflection Semantics\n+\n+Every SLURM job operates on exactly the required `--puzzle` id.\n+\n+- `off` runs the original puzzle once.\n+- `after` runs the original puzzle, constructs the synthetic reflected state\n+  from its validated solution, clears only the job-owned history, runs the\n+  reflected puzzle, inverts the reflected result, and validates it against the\n+  original initial state.\n+- `only` validates the supplied original solution, constructs the reflected\n+  state, runs only that state, inverts the result, and validates it against the\n+  original initial state.\n+\n+These modes reuse the existing cluster behavior and formulas. The portable\n+wrapper must not silently switch modes, continue after an invalid original\n+solution, or publish an unvalidated path.\n+\n+## 8. Artifacts, Validation, and Publication\n+\n+Each submission creates an immutable run directory containing the effective\n+configuration, SLURM id, solver/release manifest, hardware inventory, per-rank\n+logs, depth statistics, raw solver outputs, CPU replay report, selected original\n+facing solution, and publication receipt. Requested beam and aligned effective\n+beam are both recorded.\n+\n+The CPU validator independently replays the named Megaminx moves against the\n+packaged puzzle definition. Only validated solutions are serialized using the\n+existing CayleyPy result schema and sent to `POST /v1/results` on the existing\n+Cloudflare results-ingest Worker. The Worker remains responsible for schema and\n+replay validation, transient queue/storage handling, GitHub App authentication,\n+and append-only GitHub publication.\n+\n+Solve success and publication success are separate states. A network or Worker\n+failure never deletes or invalidates a solved local run. `--publish-only`\n+revalidates hashes and replay before resubmission, uses the stable submission\n+identity for idempotency, and records the new receipt.\n+\n+## 9. Failure and Cleanup Policy\n+\n+Errors name the failing stage and an actionable next step. Unsupported SM,\n+mixed architectures, missing `--puzzle`, invalid reflection input, insufficient\n+VRAM/scratch, corrupt archive content, missing runtime libraries, torchrun/NCCL\n+failure, and invalid replay all return nonzero.\n+\n+Cleanup is restricted to paths created under the run directory. Successful\n+runs may remove job-owned transient history after durable artifacts are written.\n+Failed runs retain history and logs for diagnosis. The launcher never removes\n+the archive, another run, a repository checkout, shared cluster dependencies,\n+or arbitrary paths resolved from unchecked environment variables.\n+\n+## 10. Branch and Release Boundary\n+\n+Development occurs on `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release` in an isolated\n+worktree based on `origin/main`. The branch contains only portable launch and\n+packaging code, release automation, tests, documentation, and required project\n+history entries. Native archives are GitHub Release assets rather than tracked\n+Git blobs.\n+\n+The existing uncommitted main-worktree changes, temporary scripts, caches,\n+build trees, private paths, and unrelated experiments must not enter the branch\n+or archives.\n+\n+## 11. Verification and Release Gates\n+\n+Required automated gates are:\n+\n+- CLI parsing and mandatory-option tests, including proof that missing\n+  `--puzzle` performs zero `sbatch` calls;\n+- shell syntax and ShellCheck;\n+- mock-SLURM tests for GPU count, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, job id parsing,\n+  torchrun world size, and all reflection modes;\n+- negative tests for unknown SM, mixed SM, insufficient resources, corrupt\n+  manifests, and missing libraries;\n+- `cuobjdump` checks for exactly one requested native SM and no PTX;\n+- archive allowlist and secret/private-path scan;\n+- CPU replay fixtures for original, after, and only modes;\n+- mocked Worker tests for accepted, duplicate, retryable, and rejected receipts;\n+- a bounded staging Worker publication test;\n+- one real smoke per architecture on its target GPU before that archive is\n+  promoted as supported;\n+- verification that the accepted staging record reaches the GitHub results\n+  repository.\n+\n+An architecture without a real target-GPU smoke may be published only as an\n+explicitly unverified prerelease asset, never as a supported release.\n+\n+## 12. Delivery Sequence\n+\n+1. Add the thin CLI, job payload, manifest format, and deterministic tests.\n+2. Adapt the current one-puzzle/reflection orchestration without changing the\n+   solver data plane.\n+3. Reuse the existing result-envelope publisher and staging Worker contract.\n+4. Add hermetic per-SM build and packaging automation.\n+5. Build and inspect all six archives.\n+6. Run target-GPU smoke tests on T4, A100, RTX 30, RTX 40/L4, H100, and RTX\n+   Blackwell as those machines are available.\n+7. Publish only passing artifacts to a GitHub prerelease, complete the\n+   end-to-end Worker-to-GitHub check, then promote the verified release.\n*** Update File: memory/PROMPTS.md\n@@\n # Prompt History\n+\n+## 2026-08-01\n+- User requested a separate minimal branch for other people to run the current\n+  Megaminx solver on their own SLURM clusters without compiling locally. # Changelog

## 2026-06-20
- Added `BEAM_SOLVE_BUCKET_MODE` for ordinary solve-to-center runs that keep
  searching after the first hit. Stream2 can now record solved candidates
  without setting `stop_flag`; the runner completes the hit depth, completes
  `BEAM_SOLVE_BUCKET_EXTRA_DEPTHS` more depths, gathers solved buckets from all
  ranks, reconstructs every stored solution, and writes per-length summaries.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_bucket.sh`, a 8-GPU IHES launcher that
  selects known length-23 puzzles from `solv_uniq.csv`, runs original and
  reflected solve-bucket passes, and writes a compact TSV with the best found
  original-facing solution for each variant.
- Changed resident solution-repair task ordering to process higher puzzle ids
  first. Parsed solution rows are now stably sorted by descending `puzzle_id`,
  so `1000, 999, ...` are prioritized while preserving per-puzzle solution
  order.
- Added an explicit `BEAM_STREAM1_MODE=uniform` runtime mode for repair/debug
  runs. In this mode Stream1 model inference is not launched; the captured
  ring-slot graph fills the score ring with zero scores so selection behaves as
  an unscored BFS frontier subject only to the configured beam width and
  dedup/final materialization.
- Fixed distributed repair result reconstruction so rank 0 is always the
  reconstruction controller after a global solved stop. Previously rank 0 could
  write `segment_solved=1` with `segment_len=-1` when another rank owned the
  solved candidate, which made resident repair TSV aggregation report false

Objective\n+\n+Publish a minimal branch and native GitHub Release artifacts that let a user\n+run one Megaminx puzzle through the existing SLURM plus torchrun production\n+path without compiling on the cluster. The normal command is:\n+\n+```bash\n+./run.sh --gpus 0,1,2,3 --beam 1000000000 --puzzle 900 --reflect off\n+```\n+\n+The user selects GPU ids, global beam width, one puzzle id, and whether to run\n+the existing reflected workflow. The launcher submits one SLURM job, preserves\n+local artifacts, validates every reported solution, and submits valid results\n+to the existing Cloudflare results-ingest Worker for GitHub publication.\n+\n+## 2. Scope and Non-goals\n+\n+The release preserves the current solver algorithms and execution semantics.\n+It does not change Stream 1-5, runtime memory contracts, history semantics,\n+reflection mathematics, result schema, or Worker publication architecture.\n+\n+The following are explicitly out of scope:\n+\n+- Docker or other container runtimes on the cluster.\n+- Compilation, CUTLASS checkout, CUDA toolkit installation, or model export on\n+  the cluster.\n+- PTX, driver JIT, cross-architecture fallback, or automatic downgrade.\n+- Mixed-SM ranks within one torchrun job.\n+- Multiple puzzles in one submitted job.\n+- Worker-side task allocation; the existing Worker remains results ingest.\n+- Direct GitHub credentials on a user cluster.\n+\n+## 3. Supported Native Artifacts\n+\n+Release automation produces one Linux x86_64 archive for each native target:\n+\n+| Archive target | Intended GPUs |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `sm75` | T4 |\n+| `sm80` | A100, including 40 GB |\n+| `sm86` | RTX 30-series |\n+| `sm89` | RTX 40-series and L4 |\n+| `sm90` | H100 |\n+| `sm120` | RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB class |\n+\n+Each production runner contains cubin for exactly its named SM. It contains no\n+PTX. A release gate inspects the executable with `cuobjdump` and rejects an\n+archive that contains PTX, lacks the requested cubin, or contains another SM.\n+An unknown or mismatched device fails before `sbatch` when visible on the login\n+node and again inside the allocation before torchrun.\n+\n+The baseline platform is a current x86_64 Linux distribution compatible with\n+Ubuntu 22.04 or newer. The archive includes the solver's redistributable runtime\n+libraries. It does not bundle an NVIDIA driver. The manifest declares the\n+minimum driver, glibc, archive SM, build commit, dependency versions, and hashes.\n+\n+## 4. Archive Contract\n+\n+Each archive contains only the portable runtime payload:\n+\n+```text\n+megaminx-smXX/\n+  run.sh\n+  cluster.env.example\n+  bin/production_runner\n+  lib/\n+  data/\n+  weights/\n+  profiles/\n+  scripts/job.sh\n+  scripts/preflight.sh\n+  scripts/validate_and_publish.py\n+  MANIFEST.json\n+  SHA256SUMS\n+  README.md\n+```\n+\n+Source trees, compilers, CUTLASS sources, build directories, caches, benchmark\n+scratch, private paths, credentials, and historical logs are forbidden. Model\n+weights and puzzle data are versioned by SHA-256 in `MANIFEST.json`.\n+\n+## 5. User Interface\n+\n+Required options:\n+\n+- `--gpus <comma-separated ids>`: one or more local GPU ids.\n+- `--beam <positive integer>`: requested global beam width.\n+- `--puzzle <nonnegative integer>`: exactly one puzzle id.\n+\n+Omitting `--puzzle` is always an error. The launcher prints\n+`missing required --puzzle; specify one Megaminx puzzle id` and performs no\n+`sbatch` call. There is no default puzzle id.\n+\n+Optional public options:\n+\n+- `--reflect off|after|only`, default `off`.\n+- `--original-solution <path>` required only for `--reflect only`.\n+- `--depth <positive integer>` when overriding the release profile default.\n+- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving again.\n+- `--dry-run` to print the validated `sbatch` request without submitting it.\n+\n+Unknown options and invalid enum values fail closed. Secrets are neither CLI\n+arguments nor environment dumps. Cluster-specific SLURM settings live in a\n+small `cluster.env` configured once: partition, account, QoS, wall time, CPU,\n+RAM, and scratch root. Runtime solver tuning remains in signed/versioned\n+per-architecture profiles rather than normal user input.\n+\n+## 6. SLURM and torchrun Flow\n+\n+`run.sh` is a login-node submitter, not the compute payload. It validates the\n+archive hashes, CLI, requested GPU count, cluster configuration, writable\n+output path, and available commands, then submits `scripts/job.sh` with\n+`sbatch`. It prints the parsed SLURM job id and exact run directory.\n+\n+The job requests a single node and a GPU count equal to the number of ids. It\n+maps the selected allocation to `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, sets one rank per GPU,\n+and invokes `python -m torch.distributed.run --nproc-per-node=<gpu-count>\n+--no-python` around the prebuilt production runner. Rendezvous and NCCL ids are\n+unique to the SLURM job. Rank 0 streams to the main log; every other rank has a\n+separate durable log.\n+\n+Inside the allocation, preflight verifies:\n+\n+1. the exact number of visible devices;\n+2. identical compute capability equal to the archive SM;\n+3. driver/runtime compatibility and required shared libraries;\n+4. per-device VRAM and derived capacity safety for the requested beam;\n+5. local data, weight, profile, and executable hashes;\n+6. scratch and history capacity before launching ranks.\n+\n+There is no alternative execution path after a failed check.\n+\n+## 7. One-puzzle and Reflection Semantics\n+\n+Every SLURM job operates on exactly the required `--puzzle` id.\n+\n+- `off` runs the original puzzle once.\n+- `after` runs the original puzzle, constructs the synthetic reflected state\n+  from its validated solution, clears only the job-owned history, runs the\n+  reflected puzzle, inverts the reflected result, and validates it against the\n+  original initial state.\n+- `only` validates the supplied original solution, constructs the reflected\n+  state, runs only that state, inverts the result, and validates it against the\n+  original initial state.\n+\n+These modes reuse the existing cluster behavior and formulas. The portable\n+wrapper must not silently switch modes, continue after an invalid original\n+solution, or publish an unvalidated path.\n+\n+## 8. Artifacts, Validation, and Publication\n+\n+Each submission creates an immutable run directory containing the effective\n+configuration, SLURM id, solver/release manifest, hardware inventory, per-rank\n+logs, depth statistics, raw solver outputs, CPU replay report, selected original\n+facing solution, and publication receipt. Requested beam and aligned effective\n+beam are both recorded.\n+\n+The CPU validator independently replays the named Megaminx moves against the\n+packaged puzzle definition. Only validated solutions are serialized using the\n+existing CayleyPy result schema and sent to `POST /v1/results` on the existing\n+Cloudflare results-ingest Worker. The Worker remains responsible for schema and\n+replay validation, transient queue/storage handling, GitHub App authentication,\n+and append-only GitHub publication.\n+\n+Solve success and publication success are separate states. A network or Worker\n+failure never deletes or invalidates a solved local run. `--publish-only`\n+revalidates hashes and replay before resubmission, uses the stable submission\n+identity for idempotency, and records the new receipt.\n+\n+## 9. Failure and Cleanup Policy\n+\n+Errors name the failing stage and an actionable next step. Unsupported SM,\n+mixed architectures, missing `--puzzle`, invalid reflection input, insufficient\n+VRAM/scratch, corrupt archive content, missing runtime libraries, torchrun/NCCL\n+failure, and invalid replay all return nonzero.\n+\n+Cleanup is restricted to paths created under the run directory. Successful\n+runs may remove job-owned transient history after durable artifacts are written.\n+Failed runs retain history and logs for diagnosis. The launcher never removes\n+the archive, another run, a repository checkout, shared cluster dependencies,\n+or arbitrary paths resolved from unchecked environment variables.\n+\n+## 10. Branch and Release Boundary\n+\n+Development occurs on `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release` in an isolated\n+worktree based on `origin/main`. The branch contains only portable launch and\n+packaging code, release automation, tests, documentation, and required project\n+history entries. Native archives are GitHub Release assets rather than tracked\n+Git blobs.\n+\n+The existing uncommitted main-worktree changes, temporary scripts, caches,\n+build trees, private paths, and unrelated experiments must not enter the branch\n+or archives.\n+\n+## 11. Verification and Release Gates\n+\n+Required automated gates are:\n+\n+- CLI parsing and mandatory-option tests, including proof that missing\n+  `--puzzle` performs zero `sbatch` calls;\n+- shell syntax and ShellCheck;\n+- mock-SLURM tests for GPU count, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, job id parsing,\n+  torchrun world size, and all reflection modes;\n+- negative tests for unknown SM, mixed SM, insufficient resources, corrupt\n+  manifests, and missing libraries;\n+- `cuobjdump` checks for exactly one requested native SM and no PTX;\n+- archive allowlist and secret/private-path scan;\n+- CPU replay fixtures for original, after, and only modes;\n+- mocked Worker tests for accepted, duplicate, retryable, and rejected receipts;\n+- a bounded staging Worker publication test;\n+- one real smoke per architecture on its target GPU before that archive is\n+  promoted as supported;\n+- verification that the accepted staging record reaches the GitHub results\n+  repository.\n+\n+An architecture without a real target-GPU smoke may be published only as an\n+explicitly unverified prerelease asset, never as a supported release.\n+\n+## 12. Delivery Sequence\n+\n+1. Add the thin CLI, job payload, manifest format, and deterministic tests.\n+2. Adapt the current one-puzzle/reflection orchestration without changing the\n+   solver data plane.\n+3. Reuse the existing result-envelope publisher and staging Worker contract.\n+4. Add hermetic per-SM build and packaging automation.\n+5. Build and inspect all six archives.\n+6. Run target-GPU smoke tests on T4, A100, RTX 30, RTX 40/L4, H100, and RTX\n+   Blackwell as those machines are available.\n+7. Publish only passing artifacts to a GitHub prerelease, complete the\n+   end-to-end Worker-to-GitHub check, then promote the verified release.\n*** Update File: memory/PROMPTS.md\n@@\n # Prompt History\n+\n+## 2026-08-01\n+- User requested a separate minimal branch for other people to run the current\n+  Megaminx solver on their own SLURM clusters without compiling locally. The wrapper\n+never silently switches mode or publishes an unvalidated path.\n+\n+## Artifacts and publication\n+\n+Each submission creates an immutable run directory with effective config,\n+SLURM id, release manifest, hardware inventory, per-rank logs, statistics, raw\n+output, CPU replay report, selected original-facing solution, and publication\n+receipt. Requested and aligned effective beam are recorded separately.\n+\n+The CPU validator independently replays named moves using packaged puzzle data.\n+Only validated results use the existing CayleyPy schema and `POST /v1/results`.\n+The existing Worker continues to validate, queue, authenticate through its\n+GitHub App, and publish append-only GitHub records.\n+\n+Solve and publication are separate states. Network failure never deletes a\n+solved run. `--publish-only` rechecks hashes and replay, reuses stable submission\n+identity for idempotency, and records the new receipt.\n+\n+## Failure and cleanup policy\n+\n+Unsupported or mixed SM, missing `--puzzle`, invalid reflection input,\n+insufficient VRAM/scratch, corrupt content, missing libraries, torchrun/NCCL\n+failure, and failed replay return nonzero with stage and next action.\n+\n+Cleanup is limited to checked paths inside the run directory. It does not introduce Docker, cluster compilation, CUTLASS checkout, toolkit installation, model export, PTX, JIT, architecture fallback, mixed-SM ranks, multi-puzzle jobs, Worker task allocation, or GitHub credentials on user clusters.\n+\n+## Native release matrix\n+\n+One Linux x86_64 archive is produced for each native target:\n+\n+| Target | Intended GPUs |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `sm75` | T4 |\n+| `sm80` | A100, including 40 GB |\n+| `sm86` | RTX 30-series |\n+| `sm89` | RTX 40-series and L4 |\n+| `sm90` | H100 |\n+| `sm120` | RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB class |\n+\n+Each runner contains cubin for exactly its named SM and no PTX. `cuobjdump` release gates reject missing, additional, or PTX targets. Unknown, mixed, or mismatched devices fail before torchrun. The platform baseline is x86_64 Ubuntu 22.04 or newer. Redistributable runtime libraries are bundled; the NVIDIA driver is not.\n+\n+## Archive contract\n+\n+```text\n+megaminx-smXX/\n+  run.sh\n+  cluster.env.example\n+  bin/production_runner\n+  lib/\n+  data/\n+  weights/\n+  profiles/\n+  scripts/job.sh\n+  scripts/preflight.sh\n+  scripts/validate_and_publish.py\n+  MANIFEST.json\n+  SHA256SUMS\n+  README.md\n+```\n+\n+The manifest records build commit, native SM, minimum driver and glibc, dependency versions, and payload hashes. Source trees, compilers, CUTLASS, build/cache directories, private paths, credentials, and historical logs are forbidden.\n+\n+## CLI contract\n+\n+Required options are `--gpus <ids>`, `--beam <positive integer>`, and `--puzzle <nonnegative integer>`. Omitting `--puzzle` prints `missing required --puzzle; specify one Megaminx puzzle id`, returns nonzero, and performs no `sbatch` call. There is no default puzzle.\n+\n+Optional options are:\n+\n+- `--reflect off|after|only`, default `off`;\n+- `--original-solution <path>`, required only for `--reflect only`;\n+- `--depth <positive integer>` to override the profile default;\n+- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving;\n+- `--dry-run` to print the validated submission without calling `sbatch`.\n+\n+Unknown values fail closed. Cluster-specific partition, account, QoS, time, CPU, RAM, and scratch settings live in `cluster.env`; ordinary users select only run inputs. Solver tuning stays in versioned per-architecture profiles.\n+\n+## SLURM and torchrun flow\n+\n+`run.sh` runs on the login node, verifies hashes and arguments, then submits `scripts/job.sh` with `sbatch`. It prints the parsed job id and run directory. The job requests one node and as many GPUs as listed, maps the allocation to `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, and starts one rank per GPU with `python -m torch.distributed.run --nproc-per-node=<count> --no-python` around the prebuilt runner. NCCL and rendezvous ids are unique per SLURM job.\n+\n+Allocation preflight verifies exact GPU count, one identical SM matching the archive, driver/runtime compatibility, required libraries, VRAM-derived beam capacity, payload hashes, and scratch/history capacity. Failure has no alternate execution path. Rank 0 streams to the main log and other ranks retain separate logs.\n+\n+## One-puzzle and reflection behavior\n+\n+Every SLURM job operates on exactly the required puzzle id:\n+\n+- `off`: solve the original puzzle once.\n+- `after`: solve original, validate it, build the reflected state, clear only job-owned history, solve reflected, invert the result, and validate it against the original state.\n+- `only`: validate the supplied original solution, build and solve only the reflected state, invert the result, and validate it against the original.\n+\n+These modes reuse the existing cluster formulas and semantics. The wrapper never silently switches mode or publishes an unvalidated path.\n+\n+## Artifacts and publication\n+\n+Each submission creates an immutable run directory with effective config, SLURM id, release manifest, hardware inventory, per-rank logs, statistics, raw output, CPU replay report, selected original-facing solution, and publication receipt. Requested and aligned effective beam are recorded separately.\n+\n+The CPU validator independently replays named moves using packaged puzzle data. Only validated results use the existing CayleyPy schema and `POST /v1/results`. The existing Worker continues to validate, queue, authenticate through its GitHub App, and publish append-only GitHub records.\n+\n+Solve and publication are separate states. Network failure never deletes a solved run. `--publish-only` rechecks hashes and replay, reuses stable submission identity for idempotency, and records the new receipt.\n+\n+## Failure and cleanup policy\n+\n+Unsupported or mixed SM, missing `--puzzle`, invalid reflection input, insufficient VRAM/scratch, corrupt content, missing libraries, torchrun/NCCL failure, and failed replay return nonzero with stage and next action.\n+\n+Cleanup is limited to checked paths inside the run directory. Success may remove job-owned transient history after durable output is written; failure retains history and logs. The launcher never removes an archive, another run, repository checkout, shared dependency, or unchecked environment-derived path.\n+\n+## Branch and release boundary\n+\n+Development occurs on `codex/megaminx-native-cluster-release` in an isolated worktree based on `origin/main`. Only portable launch/packaging code, release automation, tests, docs, and project history belong in the branch. Native archives are GitHub Release assets, not tracked blobs. Existing uncommitted main-worktree changes, temporary scripts, caches, builds, private paths, and unrelated experiments are excluded.\n+\n+## Verification gates\n+\n+- CLI tests prove missing `--puzzle` makes zero `sbatch` calls.\n+- Shell syntax, ShellCheck, and mock-SLURM tests cover GPU count, device mapping, job-id parsing, torchrun world size, and reflection modes.\n+- Negative tests cover unknown/mixed SM, resource shortage, corrupt manifests, and missing libraries.\n+- `cuobjdump` proves exactly one native SM and no PTX.\n+- Archive allowlist and secret/private-path scans pass.\n+- CPU replay fixtures cover `off`, `after`, and `only`.\n+- Worker mocks cover accepted, duplicate, retryable, and rejected receipts.\n+- A bounded staging request and subsequent GitHub record are verified.\n+- Each supported archive receives a real smoke on its target GPU.\n+\n+An architecture without a real target-GPU smoke may be an explicitly unverified prerelease asset, never a supported release.\n+\n+## Delivery sequence\n+\n+1. Implement CLI, job payload, manifest, and deterministic tests.\n+2. Adapt current one-puzzle/reflection orchestration without data-plane changes.\n+3. Reuse the existing envelope publisher and Worker contract.\n+4. Add hermetic per-SM build and packaging automation.\n+5. Build and inspect all six archives.\n+6. Smoke T4, A100, RTX 30, RTX 40/L4, H100, and RTX Blackwell as available.\n+7. Publish passing artifacts to a prerelease, verify Worker-to-GitHub, and then promote verified assets.\n*** End Patch",
  "tool": "apply_patch"
}

>>> APPROVAL REQUEST END

"""Measured 2xT4 profile derivation and safe public-run preflight payloads."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from types import MappingProxyType
from typing import Any, Mapping

from tools.kaggle_t4_mlp_profiles import align_beam


_OPTIONAL_RUNTIME_KEYS = frozenset({
    "ring_count",
    "final_materialize_chunk_candidates",
})

_REQUIRED_RUNTIME_KEYS = frozenset(
    {
        "b_micro",
        "stream1_concurrency",
        "stream3_ring_slots",
        "shard_count",
        "shard_capacity_scale_ppm",
        "stream4_batch_candidates",
        "stream4_trigger_candidates",
        "stream4_active_sort_slots",
    }
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RuntimePlan:
    requested_beam: int
    effective_beam: int
    alignment_delta: int
    profile_power: int
    model_class: str
    local_beam: int
    parent_batch: int
    stream3_batch_candidates: int
    shard_capacity_candidates: int
    runtime: Mapping[str, int]
    cross_puzzle_profile_note: str


def _positive_int(value: object, name: str) -> int:
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int) or value <= 0:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be a positive integer; got {value!r}")
    return value


def _aligned(value: int, alignment: int = 1024) -> int:
    return ((value + alignment - 1) // alignment) * alignment


def _runtime_values(profile: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, int]:
    runtime = profile.get("runtime")
    if not isinstance(runtime, Mapping):
        raise ValueError("profile runtime must be an object")
    missing = _REQUIRED_RUNTIME_KEYS.difference(runtime)
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"profile runtime missing keys: {sorted(missing)}")
    values = {name: _positive_int(runtime[name], f"runtime.{name}<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="9039" />width,
        "effective_beam": effective_beam,
        "alignment_delta": effective_beam - beam_width,
        "profile_power": profile_power,
        "profile_anchor_beam": 2**profile_power,
        "model_class": model_class,
        **profile,
    }


def validate_manifest(
    manifest: dict[str, Any],
    state_len: int,
    num_classes: int,
    move_count: int,
) -> str:
    """Validate a Stream1 MLP manifest against the selected generator."""
    for field, expected in (("state_len", state_len), ("num_classes", num_classes)):
        actual = manifest.get(field)
        if actual != expected:
            raise ValueError(
                f"manifest {field} mismatch: observed={actual!r}, expected={expected!r}"
            )
    normalization = manifest.get("normalization")
    if normalization not in {"batchnorm_folded", "layernorm"}:
        raise ValueError(
            "unsupported MLP manifest normalization: "
            f"observed={normalization!r}, expected batchnorm_folded or layernorm"
        )
    output_dim = manifest.get("output_dim")
    if not isinstance(output_dim, int):
        raise ValueError(f"manifest output_dim must be an integer; got {output_dim!r}")
    if not isinstance(move_count, int) or move_count <= 1:
        raise ValueError(f"move_count must be an integer greater than one; got {move_count!r}")
    return _model_class(output_dim, move_count)


def supported_model_header() -> str:
    """Return the exact reader-facing model support contract."""
    return """# SUPPORTED MODELS

This notebook does not support an arbitrary PyTorch architecture. It accepts
only these checkpoint layouts already implemented by `export_stream1_mlp.py`:

1. PilgrimAttnRes-style BatchNorm MLP (`batchnorm-folded`).
2. ResMLPDistance-style LayerNorm MLP (`resmlp-layernorm`).

The exported head must have `output_dim=1` or `output_dim=move_count` (24 for
Megaminx). Every other checkpoint layout or output width fails before launch.
"""

"""Measured 2xT4 profile derivation and safe public-run preflight payloads."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from types import MappingProxyType
from typing import Any, Mapping

from tools.kaggle_t4_mlp_profiles import align_beam


_OPTIONAL_RUNTIME_KEYS = frozenset({
    "ring_count",
    "final_materialize_chunk_candidates",
})

_REQUIRED_RUNTIME_KEYS = frozenset(
    {
        "b_micro",
        "stream1_concurrency",
        "stream3_ring_slots",
        "shard_count",
        "shard_capacity_scale_ppm",
        "stream4_batch_candidates",
        "stream4_trigger_candidates",
        "stream4_active_sort_slots",
    }
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RuntimePlan:
    requested_beam: int
    effective_beam: int
    alignment_delta: int
    profile_power: int
    model_class: str
    local_beam: int
    parent_batch: int
    stream3_batch_candidates: int
    shard_capacity_candidates: int
    runtime: Mapping[str, int]
    cross_puzzle_profile_note: str


def _positive_int(value: object, name: str) -> int:
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int) or value <= 0:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be a positive integer; got {value!r}")
    return value


def _aligned(value: int, alignment: int = 1024) -> int:
    return ((value + alignment - 1) // alignment) * alignment


def _runtime_values(profile: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, int]:
    runtime = profile.get("runtime")
    if not isinstance(runtime, Mapping):
        raise ValueError("profile runtime must be an object")
    missing = _REQUIRED_RUNTIME_KEYS.difference(runtime)
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"profile runtime missing keys: {sorted(missing)}")
    values = {name: _positive_int(runtime[name], f"runtime.{name}<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="9039" />width,
        "effective_beam": effective_beam,
        "alignment_delta": effective_beam - beam_width,
        "profile_power": profile_power,
        "profile_anchor_beam": 2**profile_power,
        "model_class": model_class,
        **profile,
    }


def validate_manifest(
    manifest: dict[str, Any],
    state_len: int,
    num_classes: int,
    move_count: int,
) -> str:
    """Validate a Stream1 MLP manifest against the selected generator."""
    for field, expected in (("state_len", state_len), ("num_classes", num_classes)):
        actual = manifest.get(field)
        if actual != expected:
            raise ValueError(
                f"manifest {field} mismatch: observed={actual!r}, expected={expected!r}"
            )
    normalization = manifest.get("normalization")
    if normalization not in {"batchnorm_folded", "layernorm"}:
        raise ValueError(
            "unsupported MLP manifest normalization: "
            f"observed={normalization!r}, expected batchnorm_folded or layernorm"
        )
    output_dim = manifest.get("output_dim")
    if not isinstance(output_dim, int):
        raise ValueError(f"manifest output_dim must be an integer; got {output_dim!r}")
    if not isinstance(move_count, int) or move_count <= 1:
        raise ValueError(f"move_count must be an integer greater than one; got {move_count!r}")
    return _model_class(output_dim, move_count)


def supported_model_header() -> str:
    """Return the exact reader-facing model support contract."""
    return """# SUPPORTED MODELS

This notebook does not support an arbitrary PyTorch architecture. It accepts
only these checkpoint layouts already implemented by `export_stream1_mlp.py`:

1. PilgrimAttnRes-style BatchNorm MLP (`batchnorm-folded`).
2. ResMLPDistance-style LayerNorm MLP (`resmlp-layernorm`).

The exported head must have `output_dim=1` or `output_dim=move_count` (24 for
Megaminx). Every other checkpoint layout or output width fails before launch.
"""

The manifest records build commit, native SM, minimum driver and glibc, dependency versions, and payload hashes. Source trees, compilers, CUTLASS, build/cache directories, private paths, credentials, and historical logs are forbidden.

## CLI contract

Required options are `--gpus <ids>`, `--beam <positive integer>`, and `--puzzle <nonnegative integer>`. Omitting `--puzzle` prints `missing required --puzzle; specify one Megaminx puzzle id`, returns nonzero, and performs no `sbatch` call. There is no default puzzle.

Optional options are:

- `--reflect off|after|only`, default `off`;
- `--original-solution <path>`, required only for `--reflect only`;
- `--depth <positive integer>` to override the profile default;
- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving;
- `--dry-run` to print the validated submission without calling `sbatch`.

Unknown values fail closed. Cluster-specific partition, account, QoS, time, CPU, RAM, and scratch settings live in `cluster.env`; ordinary users select only run inputs.

+## Beam-width profile selection
+
+The launcher reuses the validated Kaggle profile-selection idea, generalized for cluster hardware. Profiles are immutable JSON registries keyed by GPU family and VRAM class, native SM, torchrun world size, model backend/class, and power-of-two beam anchor. T4 measurements are evidence only for T4 and are never presented as optimal A100, H100, L4, RTX, or Blackwell settings.
+
+For a supported hardware/world-size tuple, selection uses half-up rounding of `log2(requested_beam)` to the nearest available measured anchor. The requested beam is never replaced by the anchor. It is increased only to the distributed layout quantum `world_size * shard_count * 1024`; requested beam, anchor, effective beam, and alignment delta are all recorded.
+
+After selection, the launcher<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1096" />tes

- CLI tests prove missing `--puzzle` makes zero `sbatch` calls.
- Profile tests cover half-up `log2` boundaries, exact hardware/world-size/backend matching, requested-beam preservation, distributed alignment, capacity re-derivation, unsupported ranges, and rejection of unverified or cross-hardware profiles.
- Shell syntax, ShellCheck, and mock-SLURM tests cover GPU count, device mapping, job-id parsing, torchrun world size, and reflection modes.
- Negative tests cover unknown/mixed SM, resource shortage, corrupt manifests, and missing libraries.
- `cuobjdump` proves exactly one native SM and no PTX.
- Archive allowlist and secret/private-path scans pass.
- CPU replay fixtures cover `off`, `after`, and `only`.
- Worker mocks cover accepted, duplicate, retryable, and rejected receipts.
- A bounded staging request and subsequent GitHub record are verified.
- Each supported archive receives a real smoke on its target GPU.

An architecture without a real target-GPU smoke may be an explicitly unverified prerelease asset, never a supported release.

## Delivery sequence

1. Implement CLI, job payload, manifest, and deterministic tests.
2. Adapt current one-puzzle/reflection orchestration without data-plane changes.
3. Reuse the existing envelope publisher and Worker contract.
4. Add hermetic per-SM build and packaging automation.
5. Build and inspect all six archives.
6. Smoke T4, A100, RTX 30, RTX 40/L4, H100, and RTX Blackwell as available.
7. Publish passing artifacts to a prerelease, verify Worker-to-GitHub, and then promote verified assets.

The manifest records build commit, native SM, minimum driver and glibc, dependency versions, and payload hashes. Source trees, compilers, CUTLASS, build/cache directories, private paths, credentials, and historical logs are forbidden.

## CLI contract

Required options are `--gpus <ids>`, `--beam <positive integer>`, and `--puzzle <nonnegative integer>`. Omitting `--puzzle` prints `missing required --puzzle; specify one Megaminx puzzle id`, returns nonzero, and performs no `sbatch` call. There is no default puzzle.

Optional options are:

- `--reflect off|after|only`, default `off`;
- `--original-solution <path>`, required only for `--reflect only`;
- `--depth <positive integer>` to override the profile default;
- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving;
- `--dry-run` to print the validated submission without calling `sbatch`.

Unknown values fail closed. Cluster-specific partition, account, QoS, time, CPU, RAM, and scratch settings live in `cluster.env`; ordinary users select only run inputs.

+## Beam-width profile selection
+
+The launcher reuses the validated Kaggle profile-selection idea, generalized for cluster hardware. Profiles are immutable JSON registries keyed by GPU family and VRAM class, native SM, torchrun world size, model backend/class, and power-of-two beam anchor. T4 measurements are evidence only for T4 and are never presented as optimal A100, H100, L4, RTX, or Blackwell settings.
+
+For a supported hardware/world-size tuple, selection uses half-up rounding of `log2(requested_beam)` to the nearest available measured anchor. The requested beam is never replaced by the anchor. It is increased only to the distributed layout quantum `world_size * shard_count * 1024`; requested beam, anchor, effective beam, and alignment delta are all recorded.
+
+After selection, the launcher<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1096" />tes

- CLI tests prove missing `--puzzle` makes zero `sbatch` calls.
- Profile tests cover half-up `log2` boundaries, exact hardware/world-size/backend matching, requested-beam preservation, distributed alignment, capacity re-derivation, unsupported ranges, and rejection of unverified or cross-hardware profiles.
- Shell syntax, ShellCheck, and mock-SLURM tests cover GPU count, device mapping, job-id parsing, torchrun world size, and reflection modes.
- Negative tests cover unknown/mixed SM, resource shortage, corrupt manifests, and missing libraries.
- `cuobjdump` proves exactly one native SM and no PTX.
- Archive allowlist and secret/private-path scans pass.
- CPU replay fixtures cover `off`, `after`, and `only`.
- Worker mocks cover accepted, duplicate, retryable, and rejected receipts.
- A bounded staging request and subsequent GitHub record are verified.
- Each supported archive receives a real smoke on its target GPU.

An architecture without a real target-GPU smoke may be an explicitly unverified prerelease asset, never a supported release.

## Delivery sequence

1. Implement CLI, job payload, manifest, and deterministic tests.
2. Adapt current one-puzzle/reflection orchestration without data-plane changes.
3. Reuse the existing envelope publisher and Worker contract.
4. Add hermetic per-SM build and packaging automation.
5. Build and inspect all six archives.
6. Smoke T4, A100, RTX 30, RTX 40/L4, H100, and RTX Blackwell as available.
7. Publish passing artifacts to a prerelease, verify Worker-to-GitHub, and then promote verified assets.

- `--original-solution <path>`, required only for `--reflect only`;
- `--depth <positive integer>` to override the profile default;
- `--publish-only <run-dir>` to retry publication without solving;
